  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport json\nonto = json.load(open('/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/data/wikievents/ontology_constraints_wikievents.json'))\nviolations = []\ntotal = 0\nwith open('/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/outputs/wikievents_qwen_constrained_dryrun_v2.jsonl') as f:\n    for line in f:\n        doc = json.loads(line)\n        po = doc.get('parsed_output')\n        if not po:\n            continue\n        for ev in po.get('events', []):\n            et = ev.get('event_type', '')\n            info = onto.get('event_role_constraints', {}).get(et, {})\n            vr = info.get('valid_roles', [])\n            for arg in ev.get('arguments', []):\n                total += 1\n                role = arg.get('role', '')\n                if vr and role not in vr:\n                    violations.append((doc['doc_id'], et, role, str(vr)))\nprint('Total args: ' + str(total))\nprint('RoV violations: ' + str(len(violations)))\nfor v in violations:\n    print('  ' + v[0] + ' -- ' + v[1] + ' -- ' + v[2] + ' -- valid=' + v[3])\n\"\n"
}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"F1 evaluation for Event Extraction outputs.\\n\\nMetrics:\\n  1. Entity F1: match on (entity_text, entity_type)\\n  2. Trigger Identification F1: match on (trigger_text, event_type)\\n  3. Argument Extraction F1: match on (trigger_text, event_type, arg_text, role)\\n\\nInput: JSONL file where each line has:\\n  - parsed_output: predicted {entities, events}\\n  - gold_entities: [{text, type}, ...]\\n  - gold_events: [{trigger, event_type, arguments: [{text, role}, ...]}, ...]\\n\\nText matching is case-insensitive with whitespace normalization.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\n\\ndef normalize_text(text: str) -> str:\\n    return \\\" \\\".join(text.strip().lower().split())\\n\\n\\ndef compute_prf(gold_set: set, pred_set: set) -> dict:\\n    tp = len(gold_set & pred_set)\\n    p = tp / len(pred_set) if pred_set else 0.0\\n    r = tp / len(gold_set) if gold_set else 0.0\\n    f1 = 2 * p * r / (p + r) if (p + r) > 0 else 0.0\\n    return {\\n        \\\"precision\\\": round(p, 4),\\n        \\\"recall\\\": round(r, 4),\\n        \\\"f1\\\": round(f1, 4),\\n        \\\"tp\\\": tp,\\n        \\\"gold\\\": len(gold_set),\\n        \\\"pred\\\": len(pred_set),\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef evaluate_document(parsed: dict, gold: dict) -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Evaluate one document. Returns per-metric (gold_set, pred_set) pairs.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # ── Entity F1: (text, type) ──\\n    gold_entities = gold.get(\\\"gold_entities\\\", [])\\n    pred_entities = parsed.get(\\\"entities\\\", [])\\n\\n    gold_ent_set = set()\\n    for e in gold_entities:\\n        gold_ent_set.add((normalize_text(e[\\\"text\\\"]), e.get(\\\"type\\\", \\\"\\\")))\\n\\n    pred_ent_set = set()\\n    for e in pred_entities:\\n        pred_ent_set.add((normalize_text(e[\\\"text\\\"]), e.get(\\\"type\\\", \\\"\\\")))\\n\\n    # ── Trigger Identification F1: (trigger_text, event_type) ──\\n    gold_events = gold.get(\\\"gold_events\\\", [])\\n    pred_events = parsed.get(\\\"events\\\", [])\\n\\n    gold_trigger_set = set()\\n    for ev in gold_events:\\n        trigger = ev.get(\\\"trigger\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n        if isinstance(trigger, dict):\\n            trigger = trigger.get(\\\"text\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n        gold_trigger_set.add((normalize_text(trigger), ev.get(\\\"event_type\\\", \\\"\\\")))\\n\\n    pred_trigger_set = set()\\n    for ev in pred_events:\\n        trigger = ev.get(\\\"trigger\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n        if isinstance(trigger, dict):\\n            trigger = trigger.get(\\\"text\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n        pred_trigger_set.add((normalize_text(trigger), ev.get(\\\"event_type\\\", \\\"\\\")))\\n\\n    # ── Argument Extraction F1: (trigger_text, event_type, arg_text, role) ──\\n    gold_arg_set = set()\\n    for ev in gold_events:\\n        trigger = ev.get(\\\"trigger\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n        if isinstance(trigger, dict):\\n            trigger = trigger.get(\\\"text\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n        event_type = ev.get(\\\"event_type\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n        for arg in ev.get(\\\"arguments\\\", []):\\n            gold_arg_set.add((\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.76}}"}

    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a CRITICAL finding. Let me understand what happened:

1. **The collision fix did NOT take effect on the existing 178 docs** because the re-run used `--resume`, which skips already-processed documents. The collision fix code is present but was never applied to the 178 old outputs.

2. **AC actually worsened** from 16.6% to 19.45% because the 50 new docs (previously parse failures) have even higher AC (22.59%).

3. **The root cause of AC violations is NOT the entity_type collision** but rather the model misclassifying entity types — putting SEN where PER/ORG/GPE/SID is expected.
   
So the collision fix addresses token overlap between entity types, but if 67% of AC violations are SEN appearing in roles that don't accept it, the real issue is that the model generates SEN even when it shouldn't be valid — likely because the position detection fails and the type_mask_processor can't properly constrain the choices.

Looking at the agent's findings more carefully: the `--resume` flag reused the original 178 docs without applying the fix, while only the new 68 docs got the collision fix applied. That's why AC violations persist — the majority of the dataset still has the old, unfixed outputs. Even though my 5-doc test showed the fix working (AC dropped to ~1.1%), the full 246-doc run only partially benefited because `--resume` bypassed regenerating those first 178 docs.

I need to flag this immediately: either re-run the entire experiment from scratch without `--resume`, or delete the existing output and restart with `--resume` to force a fresh generation. Before escalating though, let me verify by checking if the first document still shows the collision artifacts the fix was meant to eliminate.

Actually, the agent's verification already confirmed it definitively—178 of the original documents have identical raw outputs between old and new runs, proving `--resume` completely skipped them. This is critical and needs to go to the Director right now.
```

Interesting — the Qwen constrained bak_before16k file has 246 lines! This means the Qwen constrained experiment had already completed 246/246 before the 16k retry. So the 16k retry likely used --resume and since all 246 docs were already processed, it may not have re-run anything... unless the retry deleted some failed docs first and then re-ran them.

Wait, but the current Qwen constrained file also has 246 lines (I confirmed this earlier). And the registry showed parse=206/246 with the note "after retry with max_new_tokens=8192". 

The backup also has 246 lines, so the 16k retry was applied to the 8k result that had 246 lines but 40 parse failures, and it generated new outputs for those 40 failed docs. Since the exact-match fix was deployed alongside the collision fix, and the 16k retry likely used --resume, the 206 already-parsed docs would've kept the old behavior without the exact-match fix — though Qwen's RoV was only 0.3% anyway, so the impact is negligible. I should check whether the current Qwen constrained output still shows that same RoV or if it's changed.

I verified in the dry-run that the exact-match fix was working correctly with zero RoV violations, but those 206 old docs from the full run might still carry the 0.3% RoV. For the 16k retry with --resume, the old 206 successfully parsed docs stayed as-is with the old code behavior, while the 40 parse-failure docs got re-run at 16k and benefited from the exact-match fix — so there's a slight improvement for the newly parsed docs, but the overall RoV probably remains around 0.3% since most of the data is from the old run. This is much less severe than what happened with Mistral.

The Mistral collision fix is what really matters here — that 16.6% AC dropping to an expected 1-2% is a massive swing that directly impacts the paper's claims about cross-tokenizer generalizability, so that re-run from scratch is the priority. I need to check whether the Qwen 16k constrained result has actually improved or gotten worse, then move on to auditing the grammar_only experiments. I should also see what's currently running on the westd-16980 GPU 0 since a new process just launched there.
